K3 and the Op

K3 PT (plaintext)

As we’ve seen, the plaintext solution to K3 can be arrived at by transposing the CT using any values, for the width or height of the 
matrix, that are divisors of both 336 and 1921, and multiply to 1922. 
 
For instance, the combination of values such as 
 
 
 4,48 
 
 8,24 
 
12,16 
 
16,12 
 
24, 8 
 
48, 4 
 
have been previously demonstrated to work. 
 
But, in fact, a whole host of combinations and their permutations will work. 
 
Some combinations, of which any permutation will work 
 
Combinations that, regardless of the permutation, do not work 
 
 4, 6, 8 
 4, 8, 6 
 6, 4, 8 
 6, 8, 4 
 8, 4, 6 
 8, 6, 4 
12, 4, 4 
 4, 3,16 
 8, 8, 3 
 8, 2,12 
 
4,4,4,3 
4,4,3,4 
4,3,4,4 
3,4,4,4 
 
8,2,4,3 
8,4,3,2 
2,3,4,8 
2,4,2,12 
3,2,4,4,2 
2,4,2,4,3 
2,4,2,6,2 
2,4,2,2,2,3 
2,2,2,2,6,2 
  1,192  (336 / 192 =  1.75) 
  2, 96  (336 /  96 =  3.5) 
  3, 64  (336 /  64 =  5.25) 
  6, 32  (336 /  32 = 10.5) 
 32,  6  (336 /  32 = 10.5) 
 64,  3  (336 /  64 =  5.25) 
 96,  2  (336 /  92 =  3.5) 
192,  1  (336 / 192 =  1.75) 
 56,  3  (336 /  56 =  6, but 192 / 56 = 3.428..) 
 
 
 
The following combination/permutations provide the base case, in which all the values are prime. 
 
 
3,2,2,2,2,2,2 
 
 
2,3,2,2,2,2,2 
 
 
2,2,3,2,2,2,2 
 
 
2,2,2,3,2,2,2 
 
 
2,2,2,2,3,2,2 
 
 
2,2,2,2,2,3,2 
 
 
2,2,2,2,2,2,3 
 
So, which combination(s) are those intended by Sanborn and Schiedt for an operative to use to solve K3? 
 
Clearly, from a practical standpoint, the use of the combinations of two values, since the solution is revealed after only two 
employments, would appear to be the most efficient. 
 
However, I’ve come across combinations that are directed from intended clues very likely to be found by an op. 
                                                 
1 Although a number of solutions had been found to K3, the earliest reference I could find on the relevant importance to K3 of the values 336 and 192 was found by doing a search on the 
Yahoo Kryptos Group archive.  On Thursday Nov 6, 2003 6:41 am (message #756), Gary Warzin mentions the relevant importance of the values 336 and 192, citing the combinations 
21x16 and 28x12.  He also, however, mentioned the combination 6x32, which does not work.  It takes him a few hours, and at 2:42 pm in a separate message, #766, the same day, he notes 
this fact.  There are earlier references (message #9), again by Gary Warzin, that mention 192, 336 and 337, but speculation in the message demonstrates that he had not yet fully 
understood the relevant importance of 336 and 192. 
2 There has been discussion as to a clue that implies the value 192.  The best I can come up with at the moment is that 192 (base 10) equals C0 (base 16, also know as Hexadecimal, or 
simply Hex).  And the shape of C0 can be found when looking at the copper plate sculpture from above, such that the 0 is the circular pool and the C is the top half of the S shape of the 
sculpture.  Granted, medicinal supplements may be required.  C0 could also be mistaken as Co, the first two letters in copper which is rather significant to Kryptos, or the symbol for the 
element Cobalt, which is used in the preparation for magnetic alloys.

2 of 42 
Layers 
 
There are five strata outcroppings, but only four are comprised of multiple layers of stone. 
 
In the front entrance, 
North Morse Outcropping 
Compass Outcropping 
Arrowhead strata 
From the North West 
From the South 
From the West 
From the West 
From the South 
 
 
 
 
 
the North Morse Outcropping has 3 layers, 
 
the Compass outcropping has 2 layers, 
 
the Arrowhead strata has 1 layer. 
 
In the courtyard, 
North Outcropping 
South Outcropping 
From the West 
From the South West 
From the South 
From the South (close up) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
the two outcroppings on each end of the pool have 4 layers each. 
 
So a person entering from the west, approaching the front entrance, would first see the Morse and Compass outcroppings. 
Then, when entering the building and then the courtyard, the two courtyard outcroppings come into view. 
 
So we have, respectively, 3,2,4,4 layers at our disposal. 
 
Each of these values are divisors of both 192 and 336. 
 
However, 
3x2x4x4 = 96. 
 
We require the values multiply to 192. 
 
So what now? 
 
Well, the latest plaintext revealed3, found tagged on the very end of K2, which I’ll refer to as K2++, 
 
 
 
X LAYER TWO 
 
could not include a more explicit reference both to layers or to two. 
 
Perhaps this is the directive to include an additional value of 2 in our combination (of layers). 
 
Thus providing the combination  3,2,4,4,2  (or any permutation of these values). 
 
 
 
3x2x4x4x2 = 192. 
 
An example of this particular combination can be found immediately on the next page. 
Further examples of various combinations/permutations follow in the Appendix. 
 
Monet Friedrich 
                                                 
3 Found by Monet Friedrich and documented in Yahoo Kryptos Group message #3719, Oct 10, 2005.
